● 1. What each part is really asking for

  Part A: "Manage my subscription" card. There's no design, so the test is
  mostly about judgment.
  - What's next: show the products, the charge date and the amount.
  - Skip next shipment: this moves nextChargeAt forward by one interval (30
    days).
  - Confirmation step: "make sure the customer means it" means the customer must
    confirm. The confirmation should show the new date so the result is
    concrete.
  - Paused and cancelled: they must look clearly different from active, with no
    skip button.
  - Mobile first: it has to work at 375px.
  - Every outcome is clear: this is the real core of Part A. For loading, slow
    responses, success, conflict and server error, the customer should always
    know what happened and what to do next.
  - Reviewers: they will read the AI conversation to see how you directed the
    work and checked it. Being in charge is part of what's being graded.

  Part B: buy box. This is pixel-matching a static screenshot.
  - Size: the PNG is really 2400×1560, which means a 1200×780 CSS viewport.
  - Layout: a square product card of about 538px sits on the left. The right
    column is about 468px wide.
    - It holds an eyebrow line, stars, the rating and a reviews link, then the
      title in Fraunces and the subtitle, then a divider.
    - Below that are two option cards. The selected one has a dark green border
      and a light green background, a "Best value" pill, and the price with the
      old price struck through.
    - It also has a native-looking "Delivered every" select, two perks with
      checkmarks, the quantity stepper and the dark green "Add to cart · $25.46"
      button, then the trust row with circle-check icons and the fine print.
  - What has to work: only choosing an option and changing the quantity.
  - Photo: product.jpg is a 1024² image on a white background. In the reference
    the bottle is smaller than the card, so the photo is inset with some
    padding, not full-bleed.

  2. Mock API traps (mockApi.ts (src/api/mockApi.ts))

  - 409s are sticky. On a conflict the server sets updatedAt to now before it
    compares (mockApi.ts:34-39 (src/api/mockApi.ts#L34-L39)). Retrying with the
    same expectedUpdatedAt will always fail again. To recover you have to call
    getSubscription() again and then retry.
  - conflict=always never succeeds. Every skip bumps updatedAt again, so even
    after a fresh load the skip fails every time. The UI needs a calm dead end,
    not an automatic retry loop.
  - Conflicts change nothing visible. Only updatedAt changes. After reloading,
    the data looks identical, so "subscription changed" can't be shown as a
    visible difference.
  - Random conflicts by default. About 1 in 3 skips fail when conflict isn't
    set. Use conflict=never to test the success path.
  - Latency is 0.4–2.5 s on both calls. A double-tap or two quick confirms can
    send two requests. The second would get a 409 rather than skipping twice,
    but the button should still be disabled while a request is in flight.
  - Error order is 404 → 409 → 400. Paused and cancelled subscriptions would get
    a 409 before the 400, but that doesn't matter if we never offer skip there.
  - Nullable fields:
    - nextChargeAt and nextChargeTotalCents are null unless the status is
      active.
    - pausedUntil is only set when paused, and cancelledAt only when cancelled.
    - The types aren't a discriminated union, so TypeScript forces null checks
      everywhere.
    - imageUrl is null for the Multivitamin, so we need a placeholder.
  - Rounding mismatch. unitPriceCents is the price before the discount. If we
    show each item's discounted price, they add up to $101.88 (2546 + 7642), but
    the API total is $101.87. Show the API's nextChargeTotalCents as the total,
    and don't show per-item discounted prices.
  - Skip doesn't change the amount. nextChargeTotalCents stays the same. The
    skip returns the updated subscription, so we can use that as the new state
    without reloading.
  - Dates are relative to now and include a time of day ("in 6 days"). Format
    them with Intl.DateTimeFormat and the user's timezone.
  - fixture=error returns a 500. An unknown fixture name returns a 404. Both
    should end in an error message with a retry.
  - State resets on reload. It lives in memory in the module, so a page reload
    undoes the skip. Fine for a mock, but don't be surprised.
  - StrictMode in dev runs the load effect twice. That's harmless for a read,
    but results from a stale request need to be ignored.
  - No resume, unpause or undo endpoint. "What to do next" for paused or
    cancelled can only be text like "contact us" or "shop again", not an action.

  3. Decisions for you

  Part A
  1. How to confirm the skip:
     - an inline two-step button ("Skip" → "Yes, skip to Dec 5" / "Keep it")
     - a native <dialog>
     - window.confirm(), which is cheap but crude

     I'd pick the inline two-step or <dialog>.
  2. What to do on a 409:
     - Automatically reload the subscription, then ask the customer to confirm
       again with the fresh data. I recommend this because "make sure they mean
       it" also applies to changed data.
     - Just show an error with a reload button.
  3. Paused and cancelled: show only the status and its date, or also a next
     step with no backing API (for example "Contact us to resume")?
  4. Item rows: show only the name and quantity, or also the list price before
     the discount?
  5. Styling: there's no CSS setup yet. Options are plain CSS / CSS modules
     (built into Vite), inline styles, or Tailwind (needs an install). Should
     Part A reuse Part B's look (Fraunces/Inter, the green and cream colors) so
     both parts feel like one brand?

  Part B

  6. Nav bar and page margin: App.tsx (src/App.tsx) adds a nav bar, and the page
     still has the browser's default 8px margin. Both push the layout off the
     reference. Should we hide the nav for part=b and reset the margin?
  7. Price with quantity: should "Add to cart · $X" multiply by the quantity?
     Should it switch to $29.95 when one-time is selected?
  8. One-time selected: does the subscribe card collapse its "Delivered every"
     select and perks? The reference only shows the subscribe state.
  9. Fine print: copy.finePrint has "30 days" and "$25.46" hard-coded, so it
     would be wrong after picking 60 or 90 days. Should we build that text from
     the selection, or keep it as given? Should it hide when one-time is
     selected?
  10. Quantity limits: the minimum is 1. Is there a maximum?
  11. Narrow screens: should Part B also be responsive below 1200px? It isn't
      required.

  Process

  12. Tests: no test runner is installed. We could add Vitest and React Testing
      Library for Part A's state logic (needs your OK to install), or rely on
      typecheck, build and a manual run through every fixture.

  4. Relevant skills and tools

  - Planning before coding: superpowers:brainstorming and writing-plans for each
    part.
  - Design: frontend-design and taste for Part A's visual direction and a
    critique pass.
  - Accessibility: there's no dedicated skill, so I'd go through a checklist by
    hand:
    - real radio inputs in a group, and a native <select>
    - labelled stepper buttons, with screen-reader text like "was $29.95" for
      the struck-through price
    - a live region announcing skip results
    - visible focus, and moving focus to the confirmation and back
    - 44px touch targets at 375px
  - Testing: test-driven-development if you approve Vitest. Otherwise npm run
    typecheck / build, plus the VS Code diagnostics tool.
  - Checking it visually:
    - run starts the dev server.
    - The Chrome browser tools are disconnected in this session. Once you
      reconnect them I can take screenshots at 375px and 1200px and compare them
      with the reference PNG.
    - As a fallback, a temporary overlay of the reference image is cheap.
  - Pixel-sampling colors: I can read pixel colors from the PNG with a read-only
    PowerShell script, so we use exact colors instead of eyeballed ones.
  - Review: /code-review on the diff, plus simplify / ponytail-review to cut
    bloat, then verification-before-completion before calling anything done.
  - Commits: commit, only when you say so.
  - Not relevant here: Figma (there's no Figma file). The GitHub connector
    failed to connect in this session, which only matters if you want me to
    create the repo for you.

  Two notes on your working tree: package-lock.json is modified and NOTES.md is
  untracked and empty. I haven't touched either.

     Part A plan, rev 3: "Manage my subscription" card

     Rev 3 changes (these override anything below)

     1. The slow timer starts when submitting begins and covers the whole
        submitting phase, including the refetch after a 409. It is cleared on
        the final transition. The fake-timer test stays.
     2. The active view adds intervalDays and discountPercent. The line "Every
        {n} days · {p}% subscriber discount" is built from them, with no
        hard-coded numbers.
     3. A visible card heading "Manage my subscription" (<h2 tabIndex={-1}>) is
        the focus fallback when Skip isn't rendered.
     4. formatDate adds year: "numeric" when the year differs from the current
        year. There's a test for a December → January skip.
     5. Token provenance:
        - Sampled from the PNG: ink, green, page, tint, text-inactive (#4A5550,
          the subtitle), muted (#6B7A70), amber text #7A5A12 and amber
          background #F6E7C4 (both from the "Best value" badge).
        - My choices: reusing the amber pair for "Paused", and --neutral-bg
          #EFEBE4, which I made up.
        - Borders: each one is exactly 2 real pixels (1 CSS px), with three
          distinct colors:
          - image card and divider: #E7E1D6, which becomes --border (Part A uses
            this)
          - option card: #E1DBCF
          - quantity stepper: #CFD6D0

          Part B will add the other two when it needs them.
     6. "Keep my shipment" is also aria-disabled while submitting.
     7. "Keep my shipment" is a secondary button with a 1px --green outline
        (11.60:1 on white). Its look never relies on --border.
     8. The December → January test uses vi.setSystemTime and explicit dates, so
        it doesn't depend on today's date.

     ├───────────────────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼────────────────────┤
     │ --text-inactive on        │ #4A5550  │            │                    │
     │ --neutral-bg (Cancelled   │ /        │            │ 6.53               │
     │ label)                    │ #EFEBE4  │            │                    │
     ├───────────────────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼────────────────────┤
     │ --tint (active label      │ #F3F6F2  │            │                    │
     │ background)               │          │            │                    │
     ├───────────────────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼────────────────────┤
     │ --page                    │ #FBF8F3  │            │                    │
     ├───────────────────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼────────────────────┤
     │                           │          │            │ ~1.3, decorative   │
     │ --border                  │ #E7E1D6  │ on white   │ only (never the    │
     │                           │          │            │ only cue for a     │
     │                           │          │            │ control)           │
     └───────────────────────────┴──────────┴────────────┴────────────────────┘

     What these numbers mean:
     - Part A muted text: it uses --muted only on the white card, never directly
       on the page background.
     - Paused and cancelled text: it uses --text-inactive, which comes in at
       7.76.
     - Part B: the reference puts its fine print (4.27) and the struck-through
       price #8A948E (3.13) on a background where they fall short. We'll deal
       with that in Part B.
     - Status labels: they always carry text, so a label never relies on color
       alone.

     UI model

     type ItemView = { sku: string; name: string; quantity: number; imageUrl:
     string | null };
     type SubscriptionView =
       | { status: "active"; items: ItemView[]; nextChargeAt: Date; totalCents:
     number; skipTo: Date;
           intervalDays: number; discountPercent: number }
       | { status: "paused"; items: ItemView[]; pausedUntil: Date | null }
       | { status: "cancelled"; items: ItemView[]; cancelledAt: Date | null };
     - toView throws only for active when nextChargeAt or nextChargeTotalCents
       is null. That case shows the load-error state.
     - Paused or cancelled with a null date shows "Paused" / "Cancelled" without
       a date.
     - totalCents is the server's nextChargeTotalCents. skipTo is nextChargeAt +
       intervalDays, used only for the panel copy. After a skip, the card uses
       the server's returned value.
     - formatDate uses Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", { weekday: "long", month:
       "long", day: "numeric" }), and adds year: "numeric" when the year isn't
       the current year. formatMoney uses Intl.NumberFormat in USD.

     State machine

     type State =
       | { kind: "loading" }
       | { kind: "loadError" }
       | { kind: "ready"; raw: { id: string; updatedAt: string }; view:
     SubscriptionView;
           phase: "idle" | "confirming" | "submitting"; slow: boolean;
           notice: null | { kind: "success" } | { kind: "conflict" } | { kind:
     "skipError" } };
     Plus inFlight = useRef(false). This is the double-submit guard. It is set
     synchronously before the await, so two clicks in the same tick can't both
     get through.

     - Mount:
       - Shows a loading skeleton.
       - Then moves to ready/idle, or to loadError for any failure (500, 404, or
         toView throwing).
       - An ignore flag in the effect cleanup handles StrictMode.
     - loadError → "Try again" goes back to loading.
     - idle → Skip (active only) goes to confirming and clears the notice.
     - confirming → "Keep my shipment" goes back to idle.
     - confirming → "Yes, skip it" (allowed only if inFlight is false):
       - The phase becomes submitting, and the button reads "Skipping…".
       - A 4s timer sets slow, which shows "This is taking longer than usual…"
         in the status region. The timer runs for the whole submitting phase,
         including the refetch after a 409 (skip plus refetch can take about 5s,
         so it can show in practice). It is cleared on the final transition or
         on unmount.
     - Success: idle, with the server's data and the success notice.
     - 409:
       - The subscription is refetched.
       - If the refetch succeeds: idle with the fresh data and the conflict
         notice. No automatic retry, and a new confirmation is required.
       - If the refetch fails: loadError.
     - Other skip errors: idle with the skipError notice.
     - After a success, Skip stays available. Each skip needs a new
       confirmation.

     Copy (fixed; never shows raw API messages)

     - Load error (500, 404, invalid data): "We couldn't load your subscription
       right now. Please try again in a moment." with a Try again button.
     - Conflict (409): "Your subscription was updated elsewhere. We've refreshed
       it. Please check the details and try again if you still want to skip."
     - Skip error: "We couldn't skip your shipment, and nothing has changed.
       Please try again."
     - Success: "Done. Your next charge is now {date}. The amount stays
       {total}."
     - Active:
       - the "Active" label
       - Next charge with {date}
       - "{total} on {date}"
       - the items (name × qty)
       - "Every {intervalDays} days · {discountPercent}% subscriber discount"
         (both from the view)
       - the Skip next shipment button
     - Confirmation panel (it replaces the Skip button; the heading and text
       come first, the buttons below):
       - the heading "Skip the {date} shipment?"
       - "Your next shipment and charge move to {skipTo}. The amount stays
         {total}."
       - the buttons Yes, skip it and Keep my shipment
     - Paused: "Paused" plus "Paused until {date}" when the date exists. Items
       are shown in --text-inactive. No total and no actions.
     - Cancelled: "Cancelled" plus "Cancelled on {date}" when the date exists.
       The rest is the same as paused.

     Mobile first and the skeleton

     - Layout: one column, full width with 16px padding, max-width: 440px,
       centered. Buttons are full width and at least 48px tall. Base text is
       16px.
     - Skeleton: it reuses the active card's layout with the same block heights
       (label, date line, total line, two 48px item rows, a 48px button).
       Swapping in the data therefore causes no layout jump. The card has
       aria-busy="true", and the status region says "Loading your
       subscription…". The shimmer is turned off under prefers-reduced-motion.

     Accessibility

     - Keyboard: native <button>s only. Visible :focus-visible ring in --green
       with a 2px offset.
     - Focus management:
       - Opening the panel focuses its heading (tabIndex=-1).
       - Keep, success, conflict and skip error all return focus to Skip, or to
         the card heading if Skip isn't shown.
       - While submitting, the buttons use aria-disabled plus the ref guard
         rather than disabled, so focus isn't lost.
     - Live regions: a role="status" region that is always mounted (loading,
       slow, success) and a role="alert" region (conflict, skip error, load
       error).
     - Contrast: see the token table. Every text pair is at least 4.5:1, except
       the --muted on page background case, which is never used in Part A.
     - Touch targets: 48px buttons, and nothing tappable under 44px.

     Tests

     - Expected text is built with formatDate and formatMoney, never hard-coded
       date strings.
     - The API is mocked with vi.mock("../api/mockApi"), and the real
       fixtures.ts provides the data.
     - The DOM is cleaned up manually in afterEach, because Vitest globals are
       off.

     subscriptionView.test.ts:
     - The active fixture gives totalCents 10187, and skipTo is 30 days after
       nextChargeAt.
     - Paused and cancelled map correctly. With a null date they don't throw and
       the date is null.
     - Active with a null nextChargeAt throws.

     SubscriptionCard.test.tsx:
     1. The skeleton shows first, then Next charge with the formatted date, the
        formatted total and both items.
     2. Paused and cancelled have no Skip button and show the status with its
        date.
     3. Skip opens the panel. Focus is on its heading, the copy names skipTo and
        the unchanged total, and the API hasn't been called.
     4. Keep closes the panel, focus returns to Skip, and the API is never
        called.
     5. Confirm calls the API once with { subscriptionId, expectedUpdatedAt }.
        Then the success notice and the new date appear, and Skip is still
        available.
     6. A 409 refetches and shows the conflict copy, the card is back in idle,
        and skip was called exactly once.
     7. A load error (500) shows the fixed copy, and Try again refetches.
     8. Two synchronous clicks on confirm send one request (the ref guard).
     9. Submitting shows "Skipping…". Advancing fake timers by 4s shows "This is
        taking longer than usual…".
     10. The card rendered inside <StrictMode> ends with exactly one card
         showing the right data and no errors.

  What changed and why

  subscriptionView.test.ts (src/part-a/subscriptionView.test.ts) has 12 tests:
  - Server total: the total is the server's nextChargeTotalCents. The fixture
    gives 10187 (adding up rounded per-item prices would give 10188), and an
    override to 12345 shows the value is passed straight through, not
    recalculated.
  - Skip date: skipTo is nextChargeAt plus intervalDays. The test sets the
    interval to 60 so a hard-coded 30 would fail.
  - New, interval and discount: intervalDays: 45 and discountPercent: 20 both
    reach the active view.
  - Items: they keep sku, name, quantity and a nullable imageUrl, and have no
    price fields.
  - Active with missing data: an active subscription with a null nextChargeAt,
    or with a null nextChargeTotalCents, throws (two separate tests).
  - Paused and cancelled: each maps its date to a Date. A missing date becomes
    null instead of throwing.
  - Dates in the current year: formatDate leaves out the year.
  - New, December to January:
    - The test fixes the clock to Dec 20, 2026 with vi.setSystemTime and uses an
      explicit charge date of Dec 28, 2026.
    - It checks that toView gives skipTo = Jan 27, 2027, and that it formats as
      "Wednesday, January 27, 2027".
    - The date strings are hard-coded here because the clock and the inputs are
      both fixed. Your no-hard-coded-dates rule was about the fixtures, which
      move with today's date, so it doesn't apply to this test.
  - Money: formatMoney turns cents into USD.

  subscriptionView.ts (src/part-a/subscriptionView.ts) contains:
  - toView:
    - It handles the API's null fields per status, so the rendering code never
      has to null-check.
    - It throws only when an active subscription is missing its next charge.
    - Item prices are left out on purpose, so the UI can't show per-item
      discounted prices that don't add up to the server total. A one-line
      comment in the code says why.
  - formatDate: it uses toLocaleDateString("en-US") and adds the year only when
    it differs from the current year.
  - formatMoney: formats cents as USD with Intl. No libraries.

  Not handled: a malformed date string from the API would show "Invalid Date". I
  left it out because the mock never produces one; say if you want it covered.

● All 14 tests pass now. One correction on point 2: the test dates are local
  midday, not midday UTC, and I recommend keeping it that way.

  1. Test file not edited after the red run: confirmed. The test file was last
  written at 12:22:38, before the red run at 12:23:52. Only subscriptionView.ts
  changed after that (12:24:57). The only test-file edits since are the two new
  tests for point 3.

  2. Clock and timezone
  - The current-year test fixes the clock. It sets the clock to Dec 20, 2026
    with vi.setSystemTime(new Date(2026, 11, 20, 12)), so it won't break in
    January.
  - The test dates are local midday, not midday UTC.
    - formatDate prints dates in the machine's timezone. A local-midday date
      therefore shows the same calendar day in every timezone.
    - Midday UTC does not: 2027-01-27T12:00:00Z prints as Thursday, January 28
      in Pacific/Kiritimati (UTC+14), as the explicit-timezone check above
      shows.
    - My first attempt to prove this by setting TZ didn't count, because Node on
      Windows ignored every TZ name except UTC. Only the explicit-timezone
      check is real evidence.
    - If you still want T12:00:00Z, it's a one-line change in each test, but it
      would make the tests fail on machines at UTC+13 or UTC+14.

  × a malformed date does not throw; it becomes null
      - "pausedUntil": null
      + "pausedUntil": Date { NaN },
  Tests  2 failed | 12 passed (14)   EXIT: 1

    same "Active subscription is missing its next charge" error, which ends in

● The brief already sets the look (Part B's cream, green, Fraunces and Inter),
  so the skill's main job here was to keep me from adding template habits on
  top. Here's the direction I'd take. It's waiting for your OK; nothing has been
  written yet.

  Layout (375px, one column)

  cream page (--page), 16px side padding, 24px top
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐  white card, 1px --border, radius 16,
  no shadow
  │ Manage my subscription      (Active) │  h2: Fraunces 600 22px --ink · status
  pill on the right
  │                                      │
  │ Next charge                          │  Inter 14 --muted
  │ Thursday, November 5                 │  Fraunces 600 28px --green   ← the
  focal point
  │ You'll be charged $101.87            │  Inter 16, amount in 600 --ink
  │ Every 30 days with your 15% discount │  Inter 14 --muted
  │ ──────────────────────────────────── │  1px --border
  │ [img] Vitamin D, 60 softgels         │  48px thumb, radius 8, 1px --border
  │       Qty 1                          │  Inter 14 --muted
  │ [ M ] Multivitamin, 30 tablets       │  no image → --neutral-bg tile with a
  Fraunces initial
  │       Qty 2                          │
  │ ──────────────────────────────────── │
  │ [       Skip next shipment         ] │  48px, white, 1px --green outline,
  --green text
  └──────────────────────────────────────┘
  - One focal point: the next charge date, set large in Fraunces green. It
    answers "what's next" at a glance. Everything around it stays quiet Inter.
  - Card style: flat, with a border and no shadow, the same as Part B's image
    card. Everything is left-aligned. On wider screens the card is just centered
    at a maximum of 440px.
  - Skip is a secondary (outline) button. Filled green is saved for "Yes, skip
    it" in the confirmation panel (step 4). The less risky action looks lighter,
    which helps against accidental skips.

  How the three statuses differ

  They differ in more than color: the pill text and the content itself change
  too.

  Active
  Status pill (sentence case, not all caps): "Active": --green on --tint (10.64)
  Big date line: "Next charge" + date in --green
  Amount and button: shown
  Item text and thumbnails: --ink, full color
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Paused
  Status pill (sentence case, not all caps): "Paused": --amber-text on
  --amber-bg
  (5.20)
  Big date line: "Paused until {date}" in --text-inactive (just "Paused" if the
  date is missing)
  Amount and button: hidden
  Item text and thumbnails: --text-inactive (7.76), thumbnails grayscale
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cancelled
  Status pill (sentence case, not all caps): "Cancelled": --text-inactive on
  --neutral-bg (6.53)
  Big date line: "Cancelled on {date}" in --text-inactive
  Amount and button: hidden
  Item text and thumbnails: same as paused

  Grayscale thumbnails use a filter, not opacity, so the text keeps its token
  color and its full contrast.

  Loading and error

  - Skeleton: the same card, the same heading and the same block heights (date
    line, amount line, two 48px item rows, a 48px button) drawn as --neutral-bg
    bars. It has a slow pulse that is turned off under prefers-reduced-motion.
    It's decorative, and the live region carries "Loading your subscription…".
  - Load error: the same card and heading, the fixed message in --ink, and one
    filled --green "Try again" button, since it's the only action.

  Tokens used

  ┌────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Token          │                Where it's used                 │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --page                 │ page background                                │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ white                  │ card                                           │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --border               │ card outline, dividers, thumbnail frames (all  │
  │                        │ decorative)                                    │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --green                │ big date, outline button, primary button,      │
  │                        │ focus ring                                     │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --ink                  │ heading, item names, amount                    │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --muted                │ small labels, only on the white card           │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --text-inactive        │ paused and cancelled content                   │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ --tint, --amber-*,     │ the status pills only (--neutral-bg is also    │
  │ --neutral-bg           │ the skeleton bars and the no-image tile)       │
  └────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Fraunces is used only for the heading, the big date and the no-image initial.
  Inter is used for everything else.

  Two copy changes from the plan, for you to decide

  1. The amount line: the plan says "{total} on {date}". Directly under the big
     date, that repeats the date. I'd write "You'll be charged $101.87" instead.

  A test I changed after the red run. One assertion failed even though the card
  was right. The sentence "You'll be charged $101.87" is split by the <strong>
  around the amount, and getByText only matches text sitting directly inside one
  element. I added a small sentence() matcher that compares a paragraph's full
  text, and used it for that one assertion. The other option was to drop the
  bold amount, which was part of the direction you approved.

  At both widths the pill has already dropped below the heading. The skeleton
  follows the same rule, so the header doesn't move when data arrives. Nothing
  overflows sideways at either width.

  The jump in the card body. The card still grows by 34px at 375px and 54px at
  320px when the data arrives:
  - The big date wraps. At 28px, "Thursday, November 5" wraps at 375px, leaving
    "5" alone on the second line. At 320px, the cadence line also wraps
    ("discount" alone).
  - Paused and cancelled look too loud. "Paused until Monday, November 23" and
    "Cancelled on Tuesday, October 20" take 2–3 lines of big serif text, which
    is a lot of weight for inactive states.

  The skeleton can't know in advance whether a date will wrap, because dates
  vary in length (weekday plus month). My suggestion:
  1. Make the active date clamp(24px, 7vw, 28px) with text-wrap: balance. Common
     dates then fit on one line at 375px, and the line breaks evenly when a
     date does wrap, with no lone "5". At 320px some dates will still wrap,
     which costs one line of growth.
  2. Set the paused and cancelled status line smaller (Fraunces 20px) and still
     in --text-inactive, so inactive states look quieter.

  Do you want me to apply that, or keep 28px and accept the wrap?

● The sentence() helper does compare the full paragraph text exactly. Here it
  is, from SubscriptionCard.test.tsx (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.test.tsx):
  const sentence = (text: string) => (_: string, el: Element | null) =>
  el?.tagName === "P" && el.textContent === text;
  It only matches a <p> whose complete text equals text with strict ===. Nothing
  is trimmed or normalized, because Testing Library doesn't normalize text for
  function matchers. Extra words, a missing word or different whitespace all
  fail it.

  Next I'll check your 9.04 contrast figure for the new pill color, then write
  the failing tests.

  Where the remaining growth comes from
  - At 375px: the date line is 34px in both states (one line) and the header is

    skeleton is shaped like an active card, the common case. The status can't be
    known before the data arrives.

    That doesn't add height compared with the skeleton, since the card shrinks
    anyway.
  - Not measured: a date with a year, like "September 30, 2027". At 320px it
    might wrap; at 375px it should fit.

  Behavior, as in the plan:
  - Skip opens the inline panel, which replaces the Skip button. Focus goes to
  its heading ("Skip the {date} shipment?", full date with weekday). The text
  names the new date and says the amount stays the same. The API is not called
  yet.
  - "Keep my shipment" closes the panel and returns focus to Skip. No API call.
  - "Yes, skip it" sends one request with { subscriptionId, expectedUpdatedAt
  }. The double-submit guard is a useRef. The button reads "Skipping…" and both
  buttons are aria-disabled, not disabled.
  - Success shows the new date from the servers response and the success
  notice. Skip is still available and each skip needs a new confirmation.
  - 409: refetch, show the conflict copy, go back to idle with the fresh data.
  No automatic retry. If the refetch fails, show the load-error state.
  - Any other skip error shows the skip-error copy and nothing changes.
  - The slow timer (4s) covers the whole submitting phase, including the
  refetch.
    Please add these tests to the plans list: focus returns to Skip after
  success, conflict and skip error; a conflict where the refetch also fails;
  and a second skip that also gets a 409, to show there is no retry loop. Use
  advanceTimersByTimeAsync for the slow-message test and restore real timers
  afterwards.

● Step 4 is done: 36/36 tests pass and typecheck is clean. I didn't change any
  test after the red run. Nothing is committed.

  Red run. I used a single run instead of two stages, as explained before the
  run: every helper the tests use already existed, so they could only fail on
  the card's missing behavior. All 11 new tests failed because the panel wasn't
  there, and the 25 earlier tests still passed.

  Green run:
  === npm test           Tests  36 passed (36)   EXIT: 0
  === npm run typecheck  tsc --noEmit            EXIT: 0
  act/console warnings in the test output: 0

  Checking that the useRef guard is what stops the double submit. I temporarily
  replaced the ref check with a check on state.phase, and the test failed
  ("expected 1 call, got 2"). After restoring the ref, all 36 pass. The test can
  tell the two apart because both clicks happen inside one act(), so React
  doesn't re-render between them. A guard based on state still sees "confirming"
  on the second click; only the ref has already flipped.

  New tests (11)
  - Opening the panel:
    - Skip opens the panel in place of the Skip button, and focus moves to its
      heading "Skip the Thursday, November 5 shipment?".
    - The panel text names the new date (with the weekday) and says the amount
      stays the same. The API is not called.
  - Keep my shipment closes the panel and returns focus to Skip, with no API
    call.
  - Success:
    - The confirmation sends exactly one request with { subscriptionId,
      expectedUpdatedAt }.
    - The big date shows the date from the server's response, the success notice
      appears, and focus returns to Skip.
    - Clicking Skip again opens a new confirmation, and the API still has only
      been called once.
  - 409:
    - The card refetches, shows the conflict message, and returns to idle with
      the refetched data. Focus goes to Skip, and skip was called exactly once.
  - Your extra cases:
    - A second skip that also gets a 409 sends the refreshed updatedAt. That
      makes 2 skip calls and 3 loads in total, so there's no retry loop.
    - A 409 whose refetch also fails shows the load-error state, and focus goes
      to the card heading.
    - Focus after success, conflict and skip error is checked inside the tests
      above rather than as separate tests.
  - Other skip errors (one test each for a 500 and a network TypeError): the
    skip-error message appears, the data doesn't change, there's no refetch, and
    focus goes to Skip.
  - Double submit: two clicks in the same tick send one request. "Skipping…" and
    "Keep my shipment" are both aria-disabled, and neither has disabled.
    Clicking Keep while the request runs is ignored.
  - Slow message, twice:
    - Nothing at 3999 ms, the message at 4000 ms, and it clears when the skip
      finishes.
    - In a second test the 409 arrives at 3 s and the message still appears at 4
      s, while the refetch runs. It clears when the conflict notice appears.
    - Both use advanceTimersByTimeAsync and switch back to real timers at the
      end of the test and in afterEach.

  Decisions that weren't in the plan
  1. The success and slow messages are visible. The role="status" region is
     hidden only while loading, because the skeleton already shows that state.
  2. A 400 is treated as a generic skip error, without a refetch. Only a 409
     triggers a refetch. The UI never offers Skip on a subscription that isn't
     active, so a 400 shouldn't happen.
  3. Invalid data in any response ends in the load-error state. That includes
     the skip response and the refetch, through one shared helper.
  4. Panel structure:
     - It's a role="group" labelled by an h3.
     - "Yes, skip it" comes first, then "Keep my shipment".
     - The background is --page, and ink text on it has more than enough
       contrast.
     - While the request runs, the cursor changes to progress; there's no
       opacity, so contrast isn't reduced.
  5. Opening the panel clears any earlier message. That's in the plan, and it's
     now tested.
  6. The slow timer is cleared on unmount, through one effect.
  7. Fake timers start after the card has loaded. Testing Library's findBy waits
     using real timers, so I only switch to fake timers right before the
     confirm click.

  What changed
  - SubscriptionCard.tsx (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx): the state machine
    (phase, slow and notice), the confirmSkip flow, focus moving after each
    transition with the card heading as fallback, and the SkipPanel component.
  - SubscriptionCard.module.css (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.module.css): .alert
    is renamed to .notice (used by both live regions), plus the panel styles
    and the cursor for aria-disabled buttons.
  - SubscriptionCard.test.tsx (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.test.tsx): the 11
    tests above and vi.useRealTimers() in afterEach.

● The live regions can't move inside the card details, because those are
  unmounted while loading and on a load error, and you want the regions always
  mounted. So my plan: keep both regions as permanent children of the card and
  change the card's order to header → content (skeleton, details or nothing) →
  live regions → action (Skip button, panel or Try again).

  That puts the notices below the items and directly above the Skip button. On a
  load error the order becomes heading → message → Try again, so that screen
  doesn't visually change.

  I'll write the order test first, plus a check that the regions keep the same
  DOM nodes across state changes.

● The failure you saw is a real bug in the app, not only in the test. Focus does
  get back to Skip, but late: in the browser, <body> can hold focus across a
  frame. I have a deterministic test for it and a one-word fix, both proven in a
  scratch copy of the project. Your project is unchanged; git status shows the
  same 3 modified files.

  My earlier "40/40" was a single run. I should have run the suite repeatedly
  before reporting it as passing.

  1. Reproduction

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │                  Run mode                  │ Failed runs │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ SubscriptionCard.test.tsx, 20× in sequence │ 2 / 20      │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Full suite, 6 at a time × 3 rounds         │ 6 / 18      │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  - Same symptom every time: focus is on <body> instead of the Skip button, or
    instead of the card heading in the case where the refetch also fails.
  - Which tests fail: 6 different tests, all of them cases where focus moves
    after an async result (success, 409, refetch failure, skip error).
  - Which tests never fail: opening the panel and "Keep my shipment", where
    focus moves straight from a click.
  - Load matters: failures are more frequent under machine load.

  2. Root cause

  How focus gets back to Skip today:
  1. When the request settles, confirmSkip sets focusNext.current = "skip"
     (SubscriptionCard.tsx:110 (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx#L110)), then
     calls setState(next).
  2. React commits. The panel unmounts, and the focused "Skipping…" button goes
     with it, so the browser moves focus to <body>. The new Skip button is
     inserted, and skipRef is attached during that same commit.
  3. Focus is moved back by a passive useEffect (SubscriptionCard.tsx:60
     (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx#L60)). For a click, React runs passive
     effects right away, which is why the panel-open and Keep cases are fine.
     For an update that arrives after an await, React runs them in a separate
     task, after the commit.

  So the ref isn't stale, and the button isn't missing. The problem is a window
  between the commit and the effect, during which focus sits on <body>. My
  instrumented probe (31 runs under load) recorded the order of events:
  29× PASS | commit: Skip in DOM=true, focusNext=skip -> passive effect runs ->
  TEST checks focus = BUTTON
   2× FAIL | commit: Skip in DOM=true, focusNext=skip -> TEST checks focus =
  BODY   (effect hadn't run yet)
  In the failing runs, findByText resolved as soon as the DOM changed, before
  React's effect task ran.

  3. In a real browser: a real bug

  I ran your unmodified code in headless Edge at 375px, with focus emulation on,
  and logged focus events and animation frames:
  - The focus drop happens on every success and conflict: focusout "Skipping…" →
    BODY (nothing), then focusin Skip 0.7–2.7 ms later.
  - At the first microtask after the drop, focus is still on BODY every time. So
    the gap spans separate tasks.
  - In one run, focus dropped in frame 105 and came back in frame 106, so an
    animation frame ran while focus was on <body>. A screen reader or keyboard
    user can be caught there. This is the unreliability you described, so a
    waitFor would only hide it.

  Proposed fix (one word): change the focus effect to useLayoutEffect. Layout
  effects run synchronously inside the commit, after the DOM changes and the
  refs are attached, and before control goes back to the browser. So there's no
  task and no frame between "focus lost" and "focus restored".

  I proved this in the scratch copy only:
  - New deterministic test. It records document.activeElement at the moment the
    new Skip button first appears in the DOM, using a MutationObserver that runs
    in the same microtask checkpoint as the DOM change. It covers success, skip
    error and conflict. This makes the assertion stricter, not looser.
    - Current code: fails 10 out of 10 runs, all 3 cases.
    - With useLayoutEffect: passes 10 out of 10.
  - Full suite with the fix, 6 at a time × 3 rounds: 18 out of 18 runs passed,
    all 43 tests, with no failures at all.
  - Browser with the fix: in all 3 runs, focus is already back on Skip by the
    first microtask after the drop, in the same frame. The focusout to <body>
    still happens for a moment inside that commit, because the focused button is
    removed, but nothing can paint or react in between.

  Plan, waiting for your OK:
  1. Red: add the deterministic test to the project's SubscriptionCard.test.tsx
     (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.test.tsx), and show it fails on the current
     code. I'd add a 4th case, a 409 whose refetch fails, so focus goes to the
     card heading. The existing assertions stay as they are.
  2. Green: change useEffect to useLayoutEffect on line 60, with its import, and
     add a one-line comment saying why.
  3. Verify: the card tests 20 times in sequence and the full suite 6 at a time
     × 3 rounds, and I'll show you the counts.

  1. Loading message for screen readers

  The code is as described above: SubscriptionCard.tsx:173
  (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx#L173) puts the text in the status region,
  :192 (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx#L192) gives it the srOnly class, and
  module.css:289-299 (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.module.css#L289-L299) hides it
  with position:absolute, 1px size and clip, with no display:none or
  visibility:hidden.

  What the browser reported while the skeleton was showing:
  "statusRegion":{"text":"Loading your
  subscription…","display":"block","visibility":"visible","clip":"rect(0px, 0px,
  0px, 0px)","width":"1px","height":"1px"}
  - The text is there and not hidden from assistive tech. VERIFIED.
  - Whether a screen reader actually announces it is uncertain. ASSUMED, and
    there are two reasons to doubt it:
    - The region is created with the text already in it. Screen readers
      generally announce changes to a live region, not its initial content.
    - The region sits inside the <section aria-busy="true">, which tells screen
      readers to hold back updates from that section until loading ends.

    This is finding M1 below.

  4. Review of everything in Part A since the starter

  Tags: VERIFIED means I ran it, measured it or tested it. VERIFIED (code) means
  it follows directly from the code but I didn't run that path. ASSUMED means I
  didn't check it.

  High

  - H1. "Try again" leaves focus on <body>. VERIFIED (code).
    - Pressing it switches to the loading state, which removes the button from
      the page.
    - retry() never sets focusNext, so nothing puts focus back, either at that
      moment or after the reload.
    - Keyboard and screen-reader users lose their place. It's the same kind of
      bug we just fixed for Skip.

  Medium

  - M1. The loading announcement probably isn't heard. ASSUMED (needs a real
    screen reader). See section 1: the region is created with its text already
    in it, inside an aria-busy="true" section.
  - M2. A request that never answers leaves the customer stuck. VERIFIED (code);
    the impact is ASSUMED, since the mock always answers within 2.5 s.
    - There's no timeout. While a skip is pending, "Skipping…" and "Keep my
      shipment" are both inert.
    - The slow message says what's happening but not what to do next, which
      INSTRUCTIONS.md requires.
    - The initial load has no slow message at all.
  - M3. Only a 409 triggers a refetch. VERIFIED (code and tests).
    - A 400 ("only active subscriptions can skip", meaning it was paused or
      cancelled elsewhere) or a 404 shows "nothing has changed, please try
      again" and keeps the stale active data with Skip still offered. Trying
      again will keep failing.
    - The mock can't produce this case. Impact ASSUMED.
  - M4. The card still grows by 20px at 320px because the cadence line wraps.
    VERIFIED. At 375px it doesn't move. Paused and cancelled cards shrink from
    the active-shaped skeleton (462 → 295 at 375px). VERIFIED earlier, and
    expected.
  - M5. Verification gaps.
    - No real screen reader (NVDA or VoiceOver), so all live-region and focus
      announcement behavior is ASSUMED.
    - No real phone or iOS Safari.
    - The planned keyboard-only pass in a real browser wasn't done. Focus
      behavior is VERIFIED only through jsdom tests and Edge's DevTools focus
      logs.

  Low

  - L1. There's no Escape key to close the confirmation panel. VERIFIED (code).
  - L2. While submitting, "Keep my shipment" looks normal but silently does
    nothing. Only the cursor changes. VERIFIED (code).
  - L3. The page has no h1; the card starts at h2. Heading order for screen
    readers is a little off. VERIFIED (code); the impact may depend on the app
    shell.
  - L4. A 404 on load shows "try again in a moment", which is misleading when
    the subscription doesn't exist. VERIFIED (code and test).
  - L5. Unverified layouts:
    - Wider screens: my one 1200px capture was invalid (the headless window
      wouldn't size correctly). ASSUMED.

      screenshotted at 375px. ASSUMED.

  Source diff. Only SubscriptionCard.tsx (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx)
  changed; the CSS is untouched. The full diff, compared with a snapshot taken
  right before these fixes, is in the last tool result. In short:
  - Fix 1, Try again focus: a new "reload" focus target in the same layout
    effect.
  - Fix 2, audible loading message: aria-busy removed, and the status text
    "Loading your subscription…" is only set after mount (announceLoading).
  - Fix 3, slow message: a slow flag on the loading state, set by a 4 s timer in
    the load effect, plus the new copy.
  - Fix 4, 400 and 404 on skip: CHANGED_ELSEWHERE = [400, 404, 409] sends these
    through the existing 409 refetch path.

  Changes to existing tests (both as agreed):
  - expect(card().getAttribute("aria-busy")).toBe("true") became
    expect(card().hasAttribute("aria-busy")).toBe(false).
  - The text of the SLOW constant changed to the new copy. The two existing slow
    tests assert against that constant and are otherwise unchanged.

  New tests (8), in a describe("review fixes") block:
  - Try again focus (3 tests):
    - Clicking Try again puts focus on the card heading straight away, then on
      Skip when the reload ends active.
    - When the reload ends paused, focus stays on the heading.
    - When the reload fails again, focus also stays on the heading.
  - Loading announcement (1 test): a MutationObserver records the DOM changes
    during render. It checks that "Loading your subscription…" arrives as a
    separate change to the status region, not as part of the initial render, and
    that the card has no aria-busy.
  - Slow message during the initial load (1 test): with fake timers, the status
    still says "Loading your subscription…" at 3999 ms and shows the new copy at
    4000 ms.
  - 400 and 404 on skip (3 tests):
    - A 400 refetches and shows the refreshed (paused) state with the
      changed-elsewhere notice. Skip is gone, skip was called once, and focus is
      on the card heading.
    - A 404 refetches and returns to idle with the refetched date. The notice
      shows, focus is on Skip, a new skip needs a new confirmation, and there's
      no retry.
    - A 404 whose refetch also fails shows the load-error message, never the raw
      API text.

  Decisions to check:
  - Try again doesn't steal focus. It moves focus to the heading and then to the
    result only if focus is still on <body> or the heading. If the customer has
    moved focus somewhere else during the reload, it stays there.
  - 400 and 404 reuse the existing conflict copy. After a 400 the refreshed card
    is paused, so "…try again if you still want to skip" reads slightly off
    there, because Skip isn't offered on a paused card. I didn't add new copy
    because you asked for nothing beyond these fixes.
  - The initial load has its own 4 s timer. It's a small timer inside the load
    effect, sharing the SLOW_AFTER_MS constant and the slow flag. Reusing the
    skip's timer reference wasn't any simpler. During loading, the slow message
    appears as visible plain text below the skeleton. That's ASSUMED: I haven't
    seen it rendered, because no dev server was allowed.

     Part B plan: pixel-matched buy box

     Context

     Part B of the test: rebuild public/reference/buy-box.png in
     src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx "as close to pixel-perfect as you can". The
     screenshot is 2400×1560 at 2×, which is a 1200×780 CSS viewport. Choosing a
     purchase option and changing the quantity must work. Add to cart does
     nothing. Part A is finished and committed by the user. This plan is for
     reading and planning only; nothing gets built until it's approved.

     Approved adjustments (these override anything below)

     1. Quantity is announced only by the <output>. It's already an implicit
        live region, so there's no separate live region.
     2. The five contrast fixes apply from the start and are not tuned back
        toward the reference colors.
     3. Comparison tooling stays in the scratchpad. No new dependencies in
        package.json.
     4. I never start servers. When one is needed, I tell you and you run it in
        your own terminal.
     5. This step is build only: pricing tests and code, then the BuyBox
        behavior tests, then BuyBox.tsx, BuyBox.module.css and the App.tsx nav
        change for part=b. No visual comparison yet.
     6. Part A files stay untouched, and that includes src/styles/tokens.css.
        The new Part B tokens are custom properties scoped to the buy box's root
        class in BuyBox.module.css, and Part A's formatMoney is only imported.
     7. To finish: npm test 5 times, then typecheck and build, the raw output, a
        git diff summary, then stop.

     1. Brief (in the brief's own words) and constraints

     - "Rebuild the product purchase box shown in public/reference/buy-box.png
       in src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx, as close to pixel-perfect as you can."
     - "The image is a 2x screenshot of a 1200px-wide browser window."
     - "The product photo, fonts, and copy (src/part-b/copy.ts) are provided;
       match spacing, sizes, and colors from the image."
     - "Selecting a purchase option and changing the quantity should work;
       adding to the cart doesn't need to do anything."
     - Must match: spacing, sizes and colors at 1200px. Fraunces and Inter are
       already loaded in index.html (Fraunces 500/600 with the optical-size
       axis; Inter 400–700). The photo is /product.jpg.
     - Free: component structure, behavior the reference doesn't show (the
       one-time state, quantity limits, narrow screens), and libraries ("You can
       add any libraries you like"). I don't plan to add any.
     - Forbidden or limited (your rules): no commits, no document files in the
       repo, no dev server without telling you first, no processes left running.
     - Copy: copy.ts stays unchanged and every visible text comes from it.
     - App.tsx: INSTRUCTIONS.md and README.md say nothing against editing it.

     Your correction about the button: confirmed, except the width is 328px, not
     322 (pixel x 1616–2272).

     The button text includes the price: "Add to cart · $25.46". The basis, from
     the white glyph runs inside the button:
     - a cluster at x 889–973.5 CSS: "Add to cart", with 3 word gaps of about
       5.5px
     - a 12px gap, a 2px-wide glyph (the "·"), then another 12px gap
     - 6 glyphs at x 1000.5–1054: "$25.46"
     - the whole text block is centered on the button's middle (972)

     The 12px gaps mean the dot is its own element with about 8px margins on
     each side, not plain spaces.

     3. Files and tokens

     - src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx: one component file with small inline-SVG icon
       helpers (stars, check, check-circle, chevron, minus, plus).
     - src/part-b/BuyBox.module.css
     - src/part-b/pricing.ts + pricing.test.ts: pure pricing logic, tests
       written first.
     - src/part-b/BuyBox.test.tsx: behavior tests.
     - src/App.tsx: the nav becomes position: fixed and at most 32px tall, only
       when part=b. Part A's card starts at y=24, which a fixed nav would cover,
       so Part A keeps the nav exactly as it is.
     - Tokens reused from src/styles/tokens.css: --page, --ink, --green,
       --on-green, --text-inactive (#4A5550), --border (#E7E1D6), --amber-text,
       --amber-bg.
     - New tokens:
       - --tint #F3F6F2 (sampled), the selected option background
       - --border-option #E1DBCF (sampled)
       - --border-control #CFD6D0 (sampled)
       - --star #D8A23A (sampled)
       - the three contrast fixes below
     - Shared money formatting: reuse formatMoney from
       src/part-a/subscriptionView.ts.

     4. State and pricing

     - State:
       - purchase: "subscribe" | "oneTime", default "subscribe"
       - frequency, one of copy.subscribe.frequencyOptions, default the first
         ("30 days")
       - quantity, an integer from 1 to 10, default 1
     - Derived values:
       - unit price: copy.subscribe.priceCents (2546, already rounded) or
         copy.oneTime.priceCents
       - total: unit × quantity, so the rounded unit price times the quantity;
         for example 3 × 2546 = 7638, not round(3 × 2995 × 0.85) = 7637
       - button label: copy.addToCart · formatted total
       - fine print: only shown for subscribe
       - the frequency doesn't change the price
     - pricing.ts, all pure:
       - unitCents(purchase)
       - totalCents(purchase, qty)
       - clampQuantity(n)
       - finePrint(frequency, totalCents): builds the text from copy.finePrint
         by replacing the default frequency ("30 days") and the default
         formatted price ("$25.46") with the current values, so no copy is
         hard-coded. A test guards that both tokens still exist in
         copy.finePrint.
     - Tests first:
       - the 7638 rounding case
       - quantity clamps at 1 and 10
       - fine print for 60 days at quantity 2 reads "…every 60 days at $50.92…"
       - fine print is hidden (null) for one-time
     - Behavior tests (BuyBox.test.tsx):
       - subscribe is selected by default
       - clicking or arrowing to one-time updates the button total, collapses
         the subscribe extras and hides the fine print
       - the + and − limits work
       - the button's accessible name includes the total

     5. Accessibility

     - Purchase options: a <fieldset> with a visually hidden <legend> ("Purchase
       options") holding two native <input type="radio">.
       - Each label row (custom ring, label, badge, price) is a <label>. The
         real input is visually hidden but focusable. Native arrow keys move
         between the options.
       - The selected card shows a :focus-visible ring through :has().
       - The select and perks sit inside the subscribe card but outside the
         <label>, so there's no interactive element nested inside a label.
       - When one-time is selected, the select and perks are not rendered
         (collapsed, as you chose).
     - Prices for screen readers: the struck price is <s> with visually hidden
       "Regular price"; the current price has visually hidden "Subscription
       price". The badge text stays "Best value" in the HTML and is uppercased
       with CSS, so screen readers read it normally.
     - Frequency: a native <select> with <label> "Delivered every".
     - Quantity stepper: a role="group" labelled "Quantity". The − and + buttons
       have the labels "Decrease quantity" and "Increase quantity". At a limit
       they use aria-disabled, not disabled, so focus isn't lost. The number is
       an <output>, which is an implicit live region. It's the only
       announcement, so there's no second live region.
     - Add to cart: a type="button" with no action.
     - Decorative elements: the stars are aria-hidden (the rating is given as
       text). The trust-row and perk icons are aria-hidden.
     - Touch targets: the radio rows are 60px or more, the stepper buttons about
       40×56, and the button 56px. The select is 33px tall, which passes WCAG
       2.2 AA (24px minimum) but is below 44px. The reviews link is inline text,
       which the target-size rule exempts.
     - Focus rings: the reference shows none. I'll add a 2px --green ring with a
       2px offset. This can't be compared with the reference, because the
       screenshot doesn't show a focus state.
     - Contrast: you chose "fix all 5 minimally". The values below are the
       minimum darkening that passes, and I'll go one step darker for a safety
       margin and re-check with the script.

     ┌────────────────────────┬─────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
     │         Place          │    Reference    │             Fix              │
     ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
     │ Eyebrow, reviews link, │ #6B7A70, 4.27:1 │ --muted-on-page ≈ #68766D    │
     │  fine print (on cream) │                 │ (4.50) or one step darker    │
     ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
     │ Struck $29.95 (on the  │ #8A948E, 2.87:1 │ ≈ #6A726D (4.55)             │
     │ tint)                  │                 │                              │
     ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
     │ Unselected radio ring  │ #9AA59E, 2.55:1 │ ≈ #8C9690 (3.05) or slightly │
     │ (on white)             │  (needs 3:1)    │  darker                      │
     └────────────────────────┴─────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

     These are kept exactly as the reference, with the reason:
     - Select border (1.36–1.48) and stepper border (1.40): the chevron and the
       −/+ glyphs (7.76) already identify these controls.
     - One-time card border (1.30): the radio identifies the option.
     - Stars (2.17): decorative, because the rating is also in text.

     6. Layout

     - Desktop first: a grid with minmax(0, 540fr) minmax(0, 468fr), a 64px gap
       and 64px side padding, max-width 1200, top padding 56. The image card is
       a square (aspect-ratio: 1) with the photo inset inside it.
     - Between 860 and 1200px: the two columns shrink in proportion.
     - Below 860px: one column, with the image first (at most 540px) and the
       details below. Below 860 the details column would drop under about 380px,
       which is too narrow for "Subscribe & save 15%", the badge and the price
       on one row (they need about 400px).

     7. Visual comparison (needs a server; I'll ask before starting it)

     - Server: you start it in your own terminal when I ask. I never start
       servers.
     - Capture: headless Edge over the DevTools protocol at 1200×780 with a 2×
       pixel ratio, so the screenshot is 2400×1560 like the reference.
     - Diff: a PowerShell (System.Drawing) script in the scratchpad compares the
       two images. It reports the mismatch percentage per row band (eyebrow,
       title, and so on) and writes a diff image and a 50% overlay image. The
       top 64 real pixels are masked, because the fixed nav is drawn there.
     - Tuning: I'll adjust the ESTIMATED values until each band is within a
       small tolerance. Fonts render a little differently than in the reference,
       so the tolerance will be set by what's achievable, not by exact
       equality.
     - Narrow screens: screenshots at 860 and 375px.

     8. Open questions and risks

     - Fixed nav on part=b: with the stacked layout on a phone, the fixed nav
       sits over content while scrolling. I'll give it the page background so
       its text stays readable.
     - Fine print: with quantity 2, I'll show the total ($50.92), not the unit
       price ("renews … at $50.92"). The brief doesn't say which is meant.
     - Font rendering: Edge and the reference may anti-alias Fraunces
       differently. The title size is ESTIMATED until the overlay confirms it.
     - Photo size: the photo's size and inset inside the 540px card are
       estimates (about 488px).
     - "2,316 reviews" target: the link has nowhere to go. I'll point it at
       #reviews and document that.
     - Rounding: the brief doesn't state a rule. I'm using your rule (rounded
       unit price × quantity).

     Verification

     1. Tests first: pricing.test.ts, then BuyBox.test.tsx, each shown failing,
        then passing.
     2. npm test 5 times in a row, then npm run typecheck and npm run build.
     3. After you OK starting a server: the diff report at 1200×780 (2×) per
        band, the overlay image, and screenshots at 860 and 375px.
     4. Stop the server and check the ports. No commits.

  Remaining differences, by cause

  1. Font anti-aliasing and rendering. Even where every word lines up within
     half a pixel, the edges of the letters differ, for example 3.12% in the
     title. Small text has the most edge relative to its area, which is why the
     fine print (10.00%) and trust row (7.85%) score worst. Presumably the
     reference came from a different browser, operating system, font version or
     hinting, so this can't be tuned away.
  2. The 5 intentional contrast changes, which also count as mismatched pixels:
     - eyebrow, reviews link and fine print use #65736A instead of #6B7A70
     - the struck price uses #687069 instead of #8A948E
     - the unselected radio ring uses #87918B instead of #9AA59E

     They add to the eyebrow, rating, fine print and both option bands. I didn't
     measure how much of each band's mismatch comes from color versus
     anti-aliasing.
  3. Leftover position errors of 0.5–1.5 CSS px. Round 3 overshot upward from
     the divider down. Three more one-line tweaks would likely remove it, but
     they're beyond the 3-round limit:
     - divider margin +0.5
     - subscribe extras margin −3 → −2
     - fine print margin +1
  4. Small shape differences:
     - the perk check icon is my own SVG and sits about 1px left of the
       reference's
     - the "2,316 reviews" underline still touches the text row; in the
       reference there's a 1px gap
     - the button corner radius is estimated at 11px
     - the eyebrow sits 0.5px high
  5. Not covered by any reference: focus rings, the one-time state, and layouts
     narrower than 1200px. The comparison also skips the top 64 real px where
     the fixed nav sits.

  6. Findings by severity

  Medium
  1. The two-column layout is too tight from 860 to about 960px. VERIFIED. At
     860 the prices cross the subscribe card border by 4.6px, and the label
     wraps to 3 lines at 920 and below. Everything still works, but it looks
     broken. The 860 stacking point was my estimate; the data points to about
     960–1000.
  2. "Add to cart · $25.46" breaks onto two lines at 860 and 320, with the dot
     left at the end of line 1. VERIFIED (screenshots).
  3. The fixed nav can cover content while scrolling, and there's no
     scroll-padding-top. VERIFIED that it covers content (the title at 320px);
     ASSUMED that keyboard focus could end up hidden under it, since I didn't
     reproduce that. Small targets like the 20px reviews link could be fully
     hidden, which WCAG 2.4.11 (Focus Not Obscured) covers.
  4. No real screen reader was used. ASSUMED behaviors:
     - The quantity announces just "2", with no "Quantity", because the <output>
       itself has no label.
     - "BEST VALUE" is exposed in capitals because of the CSS uppercase, which
       some screen readers spell out letter by letter.

  Low
  5. Part B has no <main> landmark; Part A has one. VERIFIED (accessibility
  tree).
  6. Disabled − and + look exactly like enabled ones. Only the cursor changes,
  so sighted users get no cue at the limits (quantity 10 in focus-radio.png).
  VERIFIED.
  7. The rating reads "4.8" with no "out of 5" for screen readers, since the
  stars are hidden from them. VERIFIED (accessibility tree).
  8. The image alt "Vitamin D" repeats the h1 right next to it. An empty alt or
  a more descriptive one would be better. ASSUMED (a judgment call).
  9. The "2,316 reviews" link points to #reviews, which doesn't exist. It's
  marked as a placeholder in the code. VERIFIED.
  10. The reviews link's underline touches its text row (no 1px gap like the
  reference), and the eyebrow sits 0.5px high. VERIFIED.

  match within about 1px, but the shapes differ slightly from the reference.

  reference.

  of the image, because the image card is capped at 540px and left-aligned.
  VERIFIED (numbers).
  14. 200% zoom was only simulated with a 600px viewport, not with real browser
  zoom. ASSUMED equivalent.

  Over-engineering and code smells (Low, VERIFIED by reading the code)
  15. Quantity is clamped twice: totalCents clamps again although the state is
  already clamped. clampQuantity also handles fractions and NaN, which the
  stepper can never produce.
  16. Fragile fine-print derivation: it works by string-replacing "30 days" and
  "$25.46" inside copy.finePrint. A test guards it, but it would break silently
  if the copy's wording changed.
  17. Part B imports formatMoney from Part A, so the two parts depend on each
  other. The .srOnly class is duplicated in both CSS modules.
  18. Pixel-fitting leftovers: fractional values like 7.5px, 10.75px and 17.5px
  make the CSS harder to maintain.

  # Natura Wellness frontend test

  React + TypeScript + Vite. Part A is the "Manage my subscription" card, Part B
  is the pixel-matched buy box.

  Live: TODO: Vercel URL

  ## Run

  ```
  npm install
  npm run dev        # Vite dev server
  npm test           # Vitest (vitest run)
  npm run typecheck  # tsc --noEmit
  npm run build      # tsc --noEmit && vite build
  ```

  ## URLs

  | State | URL |
  |---|---|
  | Part A, active | `/?part=a&fixture=active` (also the default: `/`) |
  | Part A, paused | `/?part=a&fixture=paused` |
  | Part A, cancelled | `/?part=a&fixture=cancelled` |
  | Part A, server error (500) | `/?part=a&fixture=error` |
  | Part A, not found (404) | any other fixture value, e.g.
  `/?part=a&fixture=missing` |
  | Part A, skip always conflicts (409) |
  `/?part=a&fixture=active&conflict=always` |
  | Part A, skip never conflicts | `/?part=a&fixture=active&conflict=never`
  (default: random, about 1 in 3) |
  | Part B | `/?part=b` |

  ## Decisions

  Part A
  - Inline confirmation panel instead of `window.confirm`. It states the new
  date and that the amount stays the same, and focus moves to its heading.
  - No optimistic updates. The card only changes after the server answers.
  - A 409, 400 or 404 on skip refetches the subscription, shows a notice and
  requires a new confirmation. There is no automatic retry.
  - Paused and cancelled subscriptions show their status and date only, with no
  actions the API doesn't support.
  - The total shown is the server's `nextChargeTotalCents`. I don't add up
  per-item prices, because the rounding differs (10188 vs 10187 cents).
  - The double-submit guard is a `useRef`, so two clicks in the same tick send
  one request.
  - Focus moves in a layout effect, so it never sits on `<body>` between a
  commit and the next frame.

  Part B
  - Total = the rounded unit price × quantity (3 × $25.46 = $76.38, not a
  rounded 3 × $29.95 × 0.85).
  - The fine print is built from the selected frequency and total, and hidden
  for one-time purchases.
  - The subscribe extras (frequency select, perks) are not rendered when
  one-time is selected, so they're not reachable by keyboard.
  - The purchase options are a native radio group in a fieldset (arrow keys work
  out of the box).
  - The layout stacks at 960px and below, centered with a max width of 540px.

  ## Deviations from the reference

  | Element | Reference | Mine | Contrast |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Eyebrow, reviews link, fine print | #6B7A70 | #65736A | 4.27 → 4.70:1 on
  #FBF8F3 |
  | Struck price | #8A948E | #687069 | 2.87 → 4.69:1 on #F3F6F2 |
  | Unselected radio ring | #9AA59E | #87918B | 2.55 → 3.25:1 on white
  (non-text, needs 3:1) |
  | Stepper −/+ at a limit | full colour | glyph at 40% opacity | visible
  disabled cue |
  | Whole page at 1200px, 2x | – | 1.69% of pixels differ (tolerance 24) | the
  contrast changes add about 0.01 points; the rest is font rendering and 0.5–1px
  offsets |

  ## Checked by hand

  - Keyboard in a real browser: Tab through both parts, arrow keys on the
  radios, Enter and Space on the stepper.

  ## Known limitations

  - Part A's card grows 20px at 320px, because the cadence line wraps.
  - The loading skeleton is shaped like an active card, so paused and cancelled
  cards shrink when data arrives.
  - After a 400 the refreshed card is paused, but the notice still says "try
  again if you still want to skip".
  - "2,316 reviews" links to a placeholder anchor (`#reviews`).
  - Some screen-reader-only strings ("Purchase options", "Regular price",
  "Quantity", "out of 5", the stepper labels) and the image alt are not from
  `copy.ts`.

  ## Time

  I went over the suggested hour. The extra time went into tests, accessibility
  and a measured visual comparison against the reference. TODO: X hours of
  focused work. For a one-hour version I would skip the pixel-diff tuning, the
  narrow-screen pass and most of the focus-management tests.

  ## How I used AI

  I directed Claude Code step by step (read, plan, build, review) and approved
  every file write. It never committed or pushed; I did. Tests were written
  first and shown failing before any implementation. When I re-ran the suite
  myself I found a flaky focus test that the AI had reported as passing, which
  led to a real accessibility fix (focus restoration moved to a layout effect).
  The full conversation is at TODO: path of the export.

  Things to check before I write it:
  - Not-found row: the 404 case ("any other fixture value") comes from
    mockApi.ts. An unknown fixture leaves the mock server empty, so loading
    returns 404.
  - The 0.01-point figure: I measured it in the session where the title was
    still at −0.02em letter-spacing (1.81% with the changes, 1.80% without). I
    haven't re-measured it after the final title setting, which is why the
    README says "about 0.01 points".
  - "Checked by hand": this lists only your manual keyboard check, as you asked.
    My own keyboard pass used scripted key events in headless Edge, not a real
    browser by hand, so I left it out.
  - Accessibility fix wording: the AI paragraph calls the focus fix a "real
    accessibility fix (focus restoration moved to a layout effect)". In the
    browser, focus did sit on <body> across a frame before the fix.

  Tell me to go ahead and I'll write this to README.md, replacing the starter
  text, and show you the diff. I won't add any other document files and won't
  commit.
